and by VolkswagenStiftung (Grant No. 93562).

Appendix A
Many-body Schrödinger equation in second quantization

Generally, baryon-baryon (BB) interactions in the
S=-2
sector
can lead to
couplings between states with identical particles and with
non-identical particles,
for example
\Sigma\Sigma\rightarrow N\Xi
. Such transitions make it not

straightforward to
properly determine the combinatorial factors of free-space two-body potentials that are embedded in the
A
-body Hamiltonian matrix elements. In this appendix, we demonstrate that
these factors can systematically be deduced by comparing
the Schrödinger equation for A-body systems with the free-space two-body Schrödinger equation, provided that
these equations are derived in a consistent way.
We show explicit examples for systems of two and three particles,
and then generalize to the
A
-baryon problems. We note that Glöckle and Miyagawa
Gloeckle:2000bt
have also derived a system of coupled-Faddeev equations for three-baryon systems taking into account full particle conversions. However it is not clear to us how to read off the involved combinatorial factors based on their equations. The authors of Ref.
Shevchenko:2007zz
have formulated the problem taking all permutations of particles explictly into account. This is however not consistent with the
approach of BB interactions used in
Haidenbauer:2015zqb
;
Polinder:2007mp
;
Nagels:2020oqo
. For directly taking these interactions into account, we therefore require to derive the combinatorial factors consistent with these interactions.

To derive the general Schrödinger equation, we will work with second quantization.
The many-body Hamiltonian then has the form,

\displaystyle{H}=\sum_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}{T}_{k_{1}^{\prime}k_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}a_{k_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{k_{1}k_{2}}\\
{k_{1}^{\prime}k_{2}^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}V_{k_{1}^{\prime}k_{2}^{\prime},k_{1}k_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}a^{\dagger}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}a_{k_{2}}a_{k_{1}},

where
k_{i}
stands for a set of quantum numbers characterizing the particle state, i.e.,
momentum, spin, isospin as well as particle species
\lambda_{i}\,(\text{N},\Lambda,\Sigma\,\text{or}\,\Xi)
. When it is
necessary to separate the particle species
\lambda_{i}
from other quantum numbers, we use
k_{i}=\lambda_{i}\,\tilde{k}_{i}
. Let us further assume that the potential matrix elements
V_{k_{1}^{\prime}k_{2}^{\prime},k_{1}k_{2}}

in Eq. (
A
)
are antisymmetric under exchanges of two indices, i.e.,
V_{k_{1}^{\prime}k_{2}^{\prime},k_{1}k_{2}}=-V_{k_{1}^{\prime}k_{2}^{\prime},k_{2}k_{1}}=-V_{k_{2}^{\prime}k_{1}^{\prime},k_{1}k_{2}}=V_{k_{2}^{\prime}k_{1}^{\prime},k_{2}k_{1}}
. Note that, there is no ordering imposed
for quantum numbers of the incoming particles
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
or of the outgoing pair

k^{\prime}_{1}
and
k^{\prime}_{2}
in Eq. (
A
).

A.1
Two-body Schrödinger equation

We start with the derivation
of the Schrödinger equation in a
two-particle basis. For that, we define the ordered two-body antisymmetrized basis states as

\displaystyle|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle\equiv a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}|0\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\big(|p_{1}\rangle|p_{2}\rangle-|p_{2}\rangle|p_{1}\rangle\big),

(43)

with the right-hand side being the states in first quantization. Here,
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
also stand for the sets of quantum numbers (momentum, spin, isospin and
particle species) describing particles 1 and 2, respectively.
The completeness relation of the basis Eq. (
43
) for bases with particle species
\lambda_{1}\neq\lambda_{2}
reads

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}\}|\equiv

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}}\int d^{3}{\tilde{p}_{1}}d^{3}\tilde{p}_{2}|\{\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{1}\lambda_{2}\tilde{p}_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{1}\lambda_{2}\tilde{p}_{2}\}|=\mathbbm{1},

(44)

where the inequality
p_{1}<p_{2}
accounts for the ordering of the states in Eq. (
43
) where the leading
sorting key is assumed to be particle species. Note that by exploiting the antisymmetry of the basis functions, the
left hand side of Eq. (
A.1
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\!\!\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}\}|

\displaystyle\!\!=\frac{1}{2}\Big\{\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}\}|+\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}\}|\Big\}

\displaystyle\!\!=\frac{1}{2}\Big\{\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}\}|+\sum_{p_{1}>p_{2}}|\{p_{2}p_{1}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{2}p_{1}\}|\Big\}

\displaystyle\!\!=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p_{1},p_{2}}|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}\}|\,.

(45)

Hence, the summation over the ordered particle species on the left hand side of Eq. (
A.1
) can be replaced by a normal summation over all particle species but with a factor of
\frac{1}{2}
. For the case of two identical particles, i.e.,
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}
, the completeness relation becomes

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int d^{3}{\tilde{p}_{1}}d^{3}\tilde{p}_{2}|\{\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{1}\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{1}\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{2}\}|=\mathbbm{1}

(46)

following similar lines.
The factor
\frac{1}{2}
can also be absorbed into the definition of
the states when one rewrites Eq. (
46
) as follows

\displaystyle\int d^{3}{\tilde{p}_{1}}d^{3}\tilde{p}_{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\{\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{1}\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{1}\lambda_{1}\tilde{p}_{2}\}|\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}=\mathbbm{1}.

(47)

Now, exploiting the anticommutator relation for the creation and annihilation operators, the kinetic and potential matrix elements in the basis Eq. (
43
) are easily obtained

\displaystyle\!\!\langle\{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}\}|{T}|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle

\displaystyle=\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{1}}{T}_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{2}}-\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{2}}{T}_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{1}}+\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{2}}{T}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{1}}-\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{1}}{T}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{2}}{t}_{p^{\prime}_{2}}-\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{1}}{t}_{p^{\prime}_{2}}+\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}{t}_{p^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{1}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{2}}{t}_{p^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle\!\!\langle\{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}\}|{V}|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big({V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{2}^{\prime},p_{1}p_{2}}-{V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{2}^{\prime},p_{2}p_{1}}-{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{1}^{\prime},p_{1}p_{2}}+{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{1}^{\prime},p_{2}p_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle=2{V}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}_{2},p_{1}p_{2}}.

(48)

In the second line of Eq. (
A.1
), we have exploited the fact that the
kinetic operator is diagonal in the momentum basis. The Schrödinger equation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{H}|\Psi\rangle={E}|\Psi\rangle,\end{split}

(49)

in the two-body basis Eq. (
43
) then reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}\langle\{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}\}|{H}|\{p_{1}p_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}\}|\Psi\rangle={E}\underbrace{\langle\{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}\}|\Psi\rangle}_{\equiv\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime})}.\end{split}

(50)

Here, it will be sufficient to consider only those components of
\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime})
with

p_{1}^{\prime}<p_{2}^{\prime}
. Since the basis states are antisymmetric,
the other components of
\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime})
with
p_{1}^{\prime}>p_{2}^{\prime}
will differ from
the ones with
p_{1}^{\prime}<p_{2}^{\prime}
by a simple phase factor.
Plugging Eq. (
A.1
) into Eq. (
50
) and using
p_{1}^{\prime}<p_{2}^{\prime}
, one
arrives at a general two-body Schrödinger equation

\displaystyle{t}_{p_{1}^{\prime}}\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime})+{t}_{p_{2}^{\prime}}\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime})+\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}2{V}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime},p_{1}p_{2}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2})

\displaystyle={E}\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}).

(51)

We note that there is a factor of
2
in front of the potential
matrix elements, which drops out for the case of the two-identical particle
basis, i.e.,
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}
. In that case, we use
\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}\rightarrow 1/2\ \sum_{p_{1},p_{2}}
and equation Eq. (
A.1
)
becomes

\displaystyle{t}_{p_{1}^{\prime}}\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime})+{t}_{p_{2}^{\prime}}\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime})+\sum_{p_{1},p_{2}}{V}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime},p_{1}p_{2}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2})

\displaystyle={E}\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}).

(52)

To better understand the prefactors of the potential matrix elements present in
Eqs. (
A.1
,
A.1
), let us consider some explicit bases. In the first example, the basis consists of two two-particle states,
one with identical particles and one with distinguishable particles, e.g.,

|\{\Lambda\Lambda\}\rangle
and
|\{N\Xi\}\rangle
. Then, the
completeness relation is obtained by combining
Eqs. (
A.1
) and (
47
)

\displaystyle\!\!\int d^{3}{\tilde{p}_{1}}d^{3}\tilde{p}_{2}\,\Big\{|\{\Xi\tilde{p}_{1}N\tilde{p}_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{\Xi\tilde{p}_{1}N\tilde{p}_{2}\}|

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\{\Lambda\tilde{p}_{1}\Lambda\tilde{p}_{2}\}\rangle\langle\{\Lambda\tilde{p}_{1}\Lambda\tilde{p}_{2}\}|\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Big\}=\mathbbm{1},

(53)

leading to the following expression for the norm of the wave function

\displaystyle\!\langle\Psi|\Psi\rangle=\!\int\!\!d^{3}{\tilde{p}_{1}}d^{3}\tilde{p}_{2}\Big\{|\Psi_{N\Xi}(\tilde{p}{{}_{1}}\tilde{p}_{2})|^{2}+|\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{\Lambda\Lambda}(\tilde{p}_{1}\tilde{p}_{2})|^{2}\Big\}.

(54)

Therefore, we absorb the
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
-factor into the amplitude of states by
introducing a new set of the wave-function components,

\displaystyle\Phi_{\Lambda\Lambda}(\tilde{p}_{1}\tilde{p}_{2})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{\Lambda\Lambda}(\tilde{p}_{1}\tilde{p}_{2});\,\,\,\Phi_{N\Xi}(\tilde{p}_{1}\tilde{p}_{2})=\Psi_{N\Xi}(\tilde{p}_{1}\tilde{p}_{2}),

(55)

so that the Schrödinger equation Eqs. (
A.1
,
A.1
)
for the two newly defined components possesses a symmetric form

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c c}2{t}_{\Lambda}+{V}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Lambda\Lambda}&\sqrt{2}{V}_{\Lambda\Lambda,N\Xi}\\[5.0pt]
\sqrt{2}{V}_{N\Xi,\Lambda\Lambda}&{t}_{\Xi}+{t}_{N}+2{V}_{N\Xi,N\Xi}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{\Lambda\Lambda}\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{N\Xi}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=E\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{\Lambda\Lambda}\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{N\Xi}\end{array}\right),

where, for readability, we have omitted the dependence on
\tilde{p}
and

\tilde{p}^{\prime}
. Similarly, for the case where the basis consists of four states
\{|\Lambda\Lambda\}\rangle
,

|\{\Sigma\Sigma\}\rangle
,
|\{\Lambda\Sigma\}\rangle
and
|\{\Xi N\}\rangle
, one analogously defines a new set of wave-function components

\displaystyle\Phi_{\Lambda\Lambda}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{\Lambda\Lambda}\,;\quad\Phi_{\Sigma\Sigma}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{\Sigma\Sigma}\,;

\displaystyle\Phi_{\Lambda\Sigma}=\Psi_{\Lambda\Sigma}\,;\qquad\Phi_{N\Xi}=\Psi_{N\Xi}\,,

(64)

for which the
Schrödinger equation again possesses a symmetric form

\displaystyle{H}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{\Lambda\Lambda}\\
\Phi_{\Sigma\Sigma}\\
\Phi_{\Lambda\Sigma}\\
\Phi_{N\Xi}\end{array}\right)=E\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{\Lambda\Lambda}\\
\Phi_{\Sigma\Sigma}\\
\Phi_{\Lambda\Sigma}\\
\Phi_{N\Xi}\end{array}\right),

with

​​
{H}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c c c c }2{t}_{\Lambda}+{V}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Lambda\Lambda}&{V}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Sigma\Sigma}&\sqrt{2}{V}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Lambda\Sigma}&\sqrt{2}{V}_{\Lambda\Lambda,N\Xi}\\
{V}_{\Sigma\Sigma,\Lambda\Lambda}&2{t}_{\Sigma}+{V}_{\Sigma\Sigma,\Sigma\Sigma}&\sqrt{2}{V}_{\Sigma\Sigma,\Lambda\Sigma}&\sqrt{2}{V}_{\Sigma\Sigma,N\Xi}\\
\sqrt{2}{V}_{\Lambda\Sigma,\Lambda\Lambda}&\sqrt{2}{V}_{\Lambda\Sigma,\Sigma\Sigma}&{t}_{\Lambda}+{t}_{\Sigma}+2{V}_{\Lambda\Sigma,\Lambda\Sigma}&2{V}_{\Lambda\Sigma,N\Xi}\\
\sqrt{2}{V}_{N\Xi,\Lambda\Lambda}&\sqrt{2}{V}_{N\Xi,\Sigma\Sigma}&2{V}_{N\Xi,\Lambda\Sigma}&{t}_{\Xi}+{t}_{N}+2{V}_{N\Xi,N\Xi}\end{array}\right)

One sees that
there is a
\sqrt{2}
-factor for the transition between states of
identical and of distinguishable particles, and a factor of
2
for the transition
between states of nonidentical particles. It is important to mention that these factors are
already included in the definition of the two-body potentials
derived from chiral EFT
Polinder:2007mp
;
Haidenbauer:2015zqb

or phenomenological models

Nagels:2020oqo
(see, e.g., Eq. (2) of
Haidenbauer:2015zqb
). We therefore denote these initial two-body potentials

{V}_{\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2},\lambda^{\prime}_{1}\lambda^{\prime}_{2}}

with an appropriate factor of
\sqrt{2}
or
2
or
1

to be our new potential
{\tilde{V}}_{\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2},\lambda^{\prime}_{1}\lambda^{\prime}_{2}}
.
Expressing in terms of the new potentials
\tilde{V}
, the Hamiltonian Eq. (
A.1
)
now has a more intuitive form

{H}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c c c c }2{t}_{\Lambda}+{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Lambda\Lambda}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Sigma\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Lambda\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Lambda,N\Xi}\\
{\tilde{V}}_{\Sigma\Sigma,\Lambda\Lambda}&2{t}_{\Sigma}+{\tilde{V}}_{\Sigma\Sigma,\Sigma\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Sigma\Sigma,\Lambda\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Sigma\Sigma,N\Xi}\\
{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Sigma,\Lambda\Lambda}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Sigma,\Sigma\Sigma}&{t}_{\Lambda}+{t}_{\Sigma}+{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Sigma,\Lambda\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Sigma,N\Xi}\\
{\tilde{V}}_{N\Xi,\Lambda\Lambda}&{\tilde{V}}_{N\Xi,\Sigma\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{N\Xi,\Lambda\Sigma}&{t}_{\Xi}+{t}_{N}+{\tilde{V}}_{N\Xi,N\Xi}\end{array}\right).

In the next step, we are going to derive a similar Schrödinger equation in a
three-body basis. Then, by comparing the obtained equation with the one for two-body
basis, we will be able to determine the corresponding combinatorial factors for the potentials in
each strangeness sector.

A.2
Three-body Schrödinger equation

We define the ordered three-body basis states in second quantization and its completeness relations as

\displaystyle|\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}\rangle\equiv a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{3}}|0\rangle;

\displaystyle\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}<p_{3}}|\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}|=\mathbbm{1}.

(84)

The kinetic and potential matrix elements in the basis Eq. (
A.2
) read

\displaystyle\,\langle\{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}^{\prime}\}|{T}|\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}\rangle=

\displaystyle\,{T}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{3}}-{T}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{2}}+{T}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{1}}

\displaystyle-{T}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{3}}+{T}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{2}}-{T}_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{1}}

\displaystyle+{T}_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{2}}-{T}_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{3}}+{T}_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{3}}

\displaystyle-{T}_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{1}}+{T}_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}}-{T}_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{2}}

\displaystyle+{T}_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}}-{T}_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{2}}+{T}_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{1}}

\displaystyle-{T}_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}}+{T}_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{1}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{2}}-{T}_{p_{3}^{\prime}p_{3}}\delta_{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}}\delta_{p_{2}^{\prime}p_{1}},

and

\displaystyle\!\!\langle\{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}^{\prime}\}|{V}|\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}\rangle=2\big\{

\displaystyle{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}+{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{3}p_{1}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{2}}+{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{1}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{3}}

\displaystyle+{V}_{p^{\prime}_{3}p^{\prime}_{1},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{1}}+{V}_{p^{\prime}_{3}p^{\prime}_{1},p_{3}p_{1}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{2}}+{V}_{p^{\prime}_{3}p^{\prime}_{1},p_{1}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{3}}

\displaystyle+{V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{3}p_{1}}+{V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2},p_{3}p_{1}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{3}p_{2}}+{V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2},p_{1}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{3}p_{3}}\big\}.

Now, projecting the Schrödinger equation Eq. (
49
) onto the state

|\{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3}\}\rangle
and
then utilizing the
completeness relation in Eq. (
A.2
), one arrives at

\displaystyle\!\!\!\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}<p_{3}}\!\!\Big\{\langle\{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3}\}|{T}|\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}|\Psi\rangle+

\displaystyle\!\!\!\langle\{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3}\}|{V}|\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}|\Psi\rangle\!\Big\}\!={E}\underbrace{\langle\{p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}p^{\prime}_{3}\}|\Psi\rangle}_{\equiv\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}^{\prime})}

Similar to the case of a two-body basis, here it will be sufficient to consider
only those components of
\Psi(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}^{\prime})

with
p^{\prime}_{1}<p^{\prime}_{2}<p^{\prime}_{3}
. With this condition, only three of the 18 kinetic terms in
Eq. (
A.2
) survive. Hence,
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}<p_{3}}\langle\{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3}\}|{T}|\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}\rangle\langle\{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\}|\Psi\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=({t}_{p^{\prime}_{1}}+{t}_{p^{\prime}_{2}}+{t}_{p^{\prime}_{3}})\Psi(p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3}).

(88)

The contributions from the potential operator are a little bit more cumbersome, but can be reduced to a compact form by
exploiting the antisymmetry properties under the exchange of two indices of the potential as well as of
the wave function. For example,
the first three terms in Eq. (
A.2
) give

\displaystyle\!\!\!\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}<p_{3}}\!\!\Big\{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})+

\displaystyle\underset{p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{1}}{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{3}p_{1}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{2}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}+\underset{p_{3}\leftrightarrow p_{1}}{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{1}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{3}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}<p_{3}}{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{p_{2}<p_{1}<p_{3}}{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{3}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\Psi(p_{2}p_{1}p_{3})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{p_{3}<p_{2}<p_{1}}\underset{p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}}{{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{3}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}}}\Psi(p_{3}p_{2}p_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}<p_{3}}{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{p_{2}<p_{1}<p_{3}}{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{p_{2}<p_{3}<p_{1}}{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p_{1}}\sum_{p_{2}<p_{3}}{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{p_{2}<p_{3}}{V}_{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{2}p_{3}}\Psi(p^{\prime}_{1}p_{2}p_{3}).

(89)

Analogously, the next three terms in Eq. (
A.2
) yield

\displaystyle\!\!\!\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}<p_{3}}\!\!\Big\{\underset{p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}}{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{3}p^{\prime}_{1},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{1}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}+

\displaystyle\,\,{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{3}p^{\prime}_{1},p_{3}p_{1}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{2}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}+\underset{p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}}{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{3}p^{\prime}_{1},p_{1}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{3}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}\Big\}

\displaystyle\,\,=\cdots=\sum_{p_{1}<p_{3}}{V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{1}p_{3}}\Psi(p_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}p_{3}),

(90)

and, the three remaining terms result in

\displaystyle\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}<p_{3}}\!\!\!\Big\{\underset{p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{3}}{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2},p_{2}p_{3}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{3}p_{1}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}+

\displaystyle\underset{p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}}{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2},p_{3}p_{1}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{3}p_{2}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}+{{V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2},p_{1}p_{2}}\delta_{p^{\prime}_{3}p_{3}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}

\displaystyle=\cdots=\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}{V}_{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2},p_{1}p_{2}}\Psi(p_{1}p_{2}p^{\prime}_{3}).

(91)

Taking into account Eqs. (
A.2
-
A.2
), the Schrödinger
equation Eq. (
A.2
) in the three-body basis Eq. (
A.2
) can be written as

\displaystyle\!\!\big({t}_{p^{\prime}_{1}}+{t}_{p^{\prime}_{2}}+{t}_{p^{\prime}_{3}}\big)\Psi({p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3}})+\sum_{p_{1}<p_{2}}\!\!2{V}_{{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2},p_{1}p_{2}}}\Psi({p_{1}p_{2}p^{\prime}_{3}})

\displaystyle\!\!+\!\sum_{p_{1}<p_{3}}\!\!2{V}_{{p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{1}p_{3}}}\Psi({p_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}p_{3}})+\!\!\sum_{p_{2}<p_{3}}\!\!2{V}_{{p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3},p_{2}p_{3}}}\Psi({p^{\prime}_{1}p_{2}p_{3}})

\displaystyle\,\,=E\Psi({p^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}_{2}p^{\prime}_{3}}),

(92)

which, as one expects, differs from the Schrödinger equation in the two-body
basis Eq. (
A.1
) by the kinetic energy of the third particle and the
two-body interactions between particles
1
-
3
and
2
-
3
. Again, the factor of

2
in front of the potential vanishes when the incoming particles are
identical and the summations include all states
\tilde{p}_{1},\tilde{p}_{2}

etc.
For illustration purposes, let us consider
Eq. (
A.2
) in an explicit basis consisting of four states,
|\{N\Lambda\Lambda\}\rangle
,
|\{N\Sigma\Sigma\}\rangle
,
|\{N\Lambda\Sigma\}\rangle
and
|\{NN\Xi\}\rangle
. The norm of the wave function
in this four-particle-state basis can be calculated as follows

\displaystyle\langle\Psi|\Psi\rangle=\int d^{3}{\tilde{p}_{1}}d^{3}\tilde{p}_{2}d^{3}{\tilde{p}_{3}}\Big\{|\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{N\Lambda\Lambda}(\tilde{p}{{}_{1}}\tilde{p}_{2}\tilde{p}_{3})|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\,|\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{N\Sigma\Sigma}(\tilde{p}_{1}\tilde{p}_{2}\tilde{p}_{3})|^{2}+\,\,|\Psi_{N\Lambda\Sigma}(\tilde{p}_{1}\tilde{p}_{2}\tilde{p}_{3})|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\,\,|\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{NN\Xi}(\tilde{p}_{1}\tilde{p}_{2}\tilde{p}_{3})|^{2}\Big\}.

(93)

Based on Eq. (
A.2
), we define a new set of wavefunction components

\displaystyle\Phi_{N\Lambda\Lambda}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{N\Lambda\Lambda}\,;\quad\Phi_{N\Sigma\Sigma}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{N\Sigma\Sigma}\,;\quad

\displaystyle\Phi_{N\Lambda\Sigma}=\Psi_{N\Lambda\Sigma}\,;\quad\Phi_{NN\Xi}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Psi_{NN\Xi}.

(94)

The Schrödinger equation Eq. (
A.2
), applying
the wave function components in
Eq. (
A.2
), now has a symmetric form,

\displaystyle{(T+V)}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\ \Phi_{N\Lambda\Lambda}\\
\Phi_{N\Sigma\Sigma}\\
\Phi_{N\Lambda\Sigma}\\
\Phi_{NN\Xi}\end{array}\right)=E\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{N\Lambda\Lambda}\\
\Phi_{N\Sigma\Sigma}\\
\Phi_{N\Lambda\Sigma}\\
\Phi_{NN\Xi}\end{array}\right),

with
{T}
being a diagonal matrix

\!\!{T}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c c c c }2{t}_{\Lambda}+{t}_{N}&0&0&0\\[4.0pt]
0&2{t}_{\Sigma}+{t}_{N}&0&0\\[4.0pt]
0&0&{t}_{\Lambda}+{t}_{\Sigma}+{t}_{N}&0\\[4.0pt]
0&0&0&2{t}_{N}+{t}_{\Xi}\end{array}\right),

(104)

and the symmetric potential matrix

\hskip 42.67912pt\resizebox{16957440}{}{
${V}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c c c c }2{\tilde{V}}_{N\Lambda,N\Lambda}+{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Lambda\Lambda}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Sigma\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Lambda,\Lambda\Sigma}+\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{N\Lambda,N\Sigma}&\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Lambda,N\Xi}\\[9.0pt]
{\tilde{V}}_{\Sigma\Sigma,\Lambda\Lambda}&2{\tilde{V}}_{N\Sigma,N\Sigma}+{\tilde{V}}_{\Sigma\Sigma,\Sigma\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Sigma\Sigma,\Lambda\Sigma}+\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{N\Sigma,N\Lambda}&\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{\Sigma\Sigma,N\Xi}\\[9.0pt]
{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Sigma,\Lambda\Lambda}+\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{N\Sigma,N\Lambda}&{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Sigma,\Sigma\Sigma}+\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{N\Lambda,N\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{N\Lambda,N\Lambda}+{\tilde{V}}_{N\Sigma,N\Sigma}+{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Sigma,\Lambda\Sigma}&\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{\Lambda\Sigma,N\Xi}\\[9.0pt]
\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{N\Xi,\Lambda\Lambda}&\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{N\Xi,\Sigma\Sigma}&\sqrt{2}{\tilde{V}}_{N\Xi,\Lambda\Sigma}&{\tilde{V}}_{NN,NN}+2\tilde{{V}}_{N\Xi,N\Xi}\end{array}\right)$}.

(105)

In the last step, we have expressed the potential
matrix elements in terms of
\tilde{V}
as given in
Eq. (
A.1
).
Eqs. (
A.2
-
105
)
define the combinatorial factors of the two-body potentials present in the three-body Hamiltonian. In following, we want to generalize this result to an
A
-body system.

A.3
A
-body Schrödinger equation

With the preparation of the
A=3
system, we are now able to generalize the
combinatorial factors to arbitrary
A
. For the kinetic energy, the generalization is
trivial and leads to the sum of the single particle kinetic energies since
no particle conversion can take place for this operator. Interactions are more involved.
To the general
A
-body matrix element
\langle\{p_{1}^{\prime}\ldots p_{A}^{\prime}\}|V|\{p_{1}\ldots p_{A}\}\rangle

of the
n
-particle interaction

V=\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\\
k_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,k_{n}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}V_{k_{1}^{\prime}\ldots k_{n}^{\prime},k_{1}\ldots k_{n}}\,a^{\dagger}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}a^{\dagger}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}\cdots a^{\dagger}_{k_{n}^{\prime}}a_{k_{n}}\cdots a_{k_{2}}a_{k_{1}}

(106)

a total of

\frac{1}{n!}\,\binom{A}{n}\,\binom{A}{n}\,n!\,n!(A-n)!

(107)

different permutations of
V_{k_{1}^{\prime}\ldots k_{n}^{\prime},k_{1}\ldots k_{n}}
contribute. Therein, the first
\frac{1}{n!}

is just from the definition of
V
. Following the same steps that lead to Eq. (
A.2
), these
terms can be rearranged such that the application to an arbitrary state
\Psi
can be written as

\displaystyle\langle\{p_{1}^{\prime}\ldots p_{A}^{\prime}\}|\Psi^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}<i_{2}\ldots<i_{n}}\sum_{p_{i_{1}}<\ldots<p_{i_{n}}}

(108)

\displaystyle\times n!\ V_{p_{i_{1}}^{\prime},\ldots,p_{i_{n}}^{\prime},p_{i_{1}},\ldots,p_{i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\times\langle\{p_{1}^{\prime}\ldots p_{i_{1}}\ldots p_{i_{n}}\ldots p_{A}^{\prime}\}|\Psi\rangle\ .

For this form, we assume that
\Psi^{\prime}
is represented using the ordered states
p_{1}^{\prime}<\ldots<p_{A}^{\prime}
.
Then only one of the
(A-n)!
different spectator permutations contributes. One of the
\binom{A}{n}

terms is needed to make the sorting on the spectator particles and on the interacting
particles independent from each other as done in Eq. (
A.2
). The other one
is explicitly taken care of by the sum over
i_{1}<i_{2}\ldots<i_{n}
.

If the interacting particles are (partly) identical, we will again replace the sum over

p_{i_{1}}<\ldots<p_{i_{n}}
by (partly) full sums and add the appropriate combinatorial factor (e.g.
1/2!
in the case of two identical particles). Note that this factors depend on the kind of particles in the
incoming
\Psi
state.

We again introduce rescaled wave functions by studying the norm of the states similar to Eq. (
A.2
). The appropriate factors for states with
p
particles
species and
n_{1},\ldots,n_{p}
particles of each species are
\sqrt{n_{1}!\ldots n_{p}!}
.
The potential matrix element needs to be multiplied with (divided by) this factor for incoming states
(outgoing states) to reexpress Eq. (
108
) in terms of
\Phi
states.
We note that the potential matrix in terms of these states is symmetrical. In summary,
the potential matrix elements then reads

\frac{n!\sqrt{n_{1}!\ldots n_{p}!}}{\sqrt{n_{1}^{\prime}!\ldots n_{p}^{\prime}!}}\ V_{p_{i_{1}}^{\prime},\ldots,p_{i_{n}}^{\prime},p_{i_{1}},\ldots,p_{i_{n}}}\ .

(109)

Note that here the factor does not include the additional factor required when identical
particles are involved in the sum of Eq. (
108
).

We then simplify the expressions by identifying
n
-particles that contribute identically
to Eq. (
108
). The sum over
i_{1}<i_{2}\ldots<i_{n}
can then be reduced and
tuples of outgoing states involving the same kind of particles can be combined by the appropriate factor.
Finally, we build the ratio of the factors for the
A
-body and
n
-body systems to find the
correct combinatorial factors that enter our J-NCSM calculations.

As an example, we now consider some selected matrix elements of the
S=-2
2-body interaction for

(A-2)
-
\Lambda\Lambda
,

(A-2)
-
\Lambda\Sigma
,

(A-2)
-
\Sigma\Sigma
,
and
(A-1)
-
\Xi
states.
For the diagonal matrix elements in particle space, the square root
factors in Eq. (
109
) cancel. In this case, the prefactor
is just
2\times
the number of pairs contributing in the outgoing channel
\times 1/2
if the active pair in the incoming channel
consists of identical particles. Therefore, for our example, we find

\displaystyle\langle(A-2)-\Lambda\Lambda|V|(A-2)-\Lambda\Lambda\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\cdot 1\cdot\frac{1}{2}\cdot V_{p_{\Lambda,1}^{\prime}p_{\Lambda,2}^{\prime},p_{\Lambda,1}p_{\Lambda,2}}=\tilde{V}_{p_{\Lambda,1}^{\prime}p_{\Lambda,2}^{\prime},p_{\Lambda,1}p_{\Lambda,2}}

\displaystyle\langle(A-2)-\Lambda\Sigma|V|(A-2)-\Lambda\Sigma\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\cdot 1\cdot V_{p_{\Lambda}^{\prime}p_{\Sigma}^{\prime},p_{\Lambda}p_{\Sigma}}=\tilde{V}_{p_{\Lambda}^{\prime}p_{\Sigma}^{\prime},p_{\Lambda}p_{\Sigma}}

\displaystyle\langle(A-2)-\Sigma\Sigma|V|(A-2)-\Sigma\Sigma\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\cdot 1\cdot\frac{1}{2}\cdot V_{p_{\Sigma,1}^{\prime}p_{\Sigma,2}^{\prime},p_{\Sigma,1}p_{\Sigma,2}}=\tilde{V}_{p_{\Sigma,1}^{\prime}p_{\Sigma,2}^{\prime},p_{\Sigma,1}p_{\Sigma,2}}

\displaystyle\langle(A-1)-\Xi|V|(A-1)-\Xi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\cdot(A-1)\cdot V_{p_{N}^{\prime}p_{\Xi}^{\prime},p_{N}p_{\Xi}}=(A-1){\tilde{V}}_{p_{N}^{\prime}p_{\Xi}^{\prime},p_{N}p_{\Xi}}\ .

(110)

In the last step, we have exploited the results of the two-body system that relate
V
to

\tilde{V}
in Eq. (
A.1
).
The resulting combinatorial factors agree with the expectation that
the interaction just has to be multiplied by the number of pairs contributing.
More interesting is the case of transitions. Here, we first look at
transitions between
\Sigma\Sigma
and
\Lambda\Sigma
states. Because
the identity of the particles changes, we now have additionally the
contribution of the square root factors. They are also important to
guarantee that the interaction matrix is symmetric.

The result for the two matrix elements is

\displaystyle\langle(A-2)-\Sigma\Sigma|V|(A-2)-\Lambda\Sigma\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\cdot 1\cdot 1\cdot\frac{\sqrt{(A-2)!}}{\sqrt{2!(A-2)!}}\ \cdot V_{p_{\Sigma,1}^{\prime}p_{\Sigma,2}^{\prime},p_{\Lambda}p_{\Sigma}}=\tilde{V}_{p_{\Sigma,1}^{\prime}p_{\Sigma,2}^{\prime},p_{\Lambda}p_{\Sigma}}

\displaystyle\langle(A-2)-\Lambda\Sigma|V|(A-2)-\Sigma\Sigma\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\cdot 1\cdot\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2!(A-2)!}}{\sqrt{(A-2)!}}\ \cdot V_{p_{\Lambda}^{\prime}p_{\Sigma}^{\prime},p_{\Sigma,1}p_{\Sigma,2}}=\tilde{V}_{p_{\Lambda}^{\prime}p_{\Sigma}^{\prime},p_{\Sigma,1}p_{\Sigma,2}}

(111)

and just reflects the number of
YY
pairs in the
A
-body state. The final examples
are transitions of
\Lambda\Lambda
and
N\Xi
. For these matrix elements,
several pairs contribute and the identity of the particles changes. It is reassuring that we also find in this case symmetry of the potential matrix elements

\displaystyle\langle(A-2)-\Lambda\Lambda|V|(A-1)-\Xi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\cdot 1\cdot 1\cdot\frac{\sqrt{(A-1)!}}{\sqrt{2!(A-2)!}}\cdot V_{p_{\Lambda,1}^{\prime}p_{\Lambda,2}^{\prime},p_{N}p_{\Xi}}=\sqrt{A-1}\ \tilde{V}_{p_{\Lambda,1}^{\prime}p_{\Lambda,2}^{\prime},p_{N}p_{\Xi}}

\displaystyle\langle(A-1)-\Xi|V|(A-2)-\Lambda\Lambda\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\cdot(A-1)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2!(A-2)!}}{\sqrt{(A-1)!}}\cdot V_{p_{N}^{\prime}p_{\Xi}^{\prime},p_{\Lambda,1}p_{\Lambda,2}}=\sqrt{A-1}\ \tilde{V}_{p_{N}^{\prime}p_{\Xi}^{\prime},p_{\Lambda,1}p_{\Lambda,2}}\ .

(112)

In this way, it is straightforward to identify all relevant combinatorial
factors for our calculations. For the
S=0
interactions, where particle
transitions do not occur, it is simply given by the number of NN pairs in
the state, i.e.
\binom{A-2}{2}
and
\binom{A-1}{2}
for
(A-2)
-YY and

(A-1)
-
\Xi
states, respectively. The factors for
S=-1
(
S=-2
) interactions
are summarized in Table
3
(Table
4
).
To shorten the presentation, we only include particle transitions in one direction. The other one is given by the symmetry of the potentials.
